> AGENT

Good — let's plan this properly before writing any code. Here is how I'd break it down.

## 1. Three actors, what we need from each

### Farmer / Seller (mostly already covered)
We have farmer profile + UPI/COD toggles. For delivery, we additionally need:
- **Pickup address** (door no, street, village, mandal, district, pincode) — separate from farm location, because the farm may be remote and pickup may happen from a packing point or shop.
- **Pickup contact** (name + phone) if different from the farmer's primary phone.
- **Pickup window** (e.g. "available 8 AM – 6 PM") so the delivery boy isn't sent at a bad time.
- **Per-listing weight / volume** (kg, or "small / medium / large carton") — needed to compute delivery fee and to tell the rider what to expect.
- **"Delivery available" toggle per farmer** — some farmers may only do pickup-by-buyer; we shouldn't force delivery on everyone.
- **Optional fragile / cold-chain flag** on a listing (curd, ghee, leafy greens spoil fast).

### Consumer / Customer
We currently capture name + phone + payment proof. We need to add:
- **Delivery address** (door no, street, area, landmark, city, pincode) — saved on the consumer profile so they don't retype.
- **Geolocation pin (lat/lng)** — optional but very useful for the rider; we already have `LocationSearch.tsx` we can reuse.
- **Delivery preference at checkout**: `pickup_self` vs `delivery`.
- **Preferred delivery slot** (today evening / tomorrow morning) — keeps expectations realistic.
- **Delivery fee acknowledgment** — they see and accept the fee before placing the order.
- **Alt contact number** (optional) — gate-watchman, spouse, etc.

### Delivery Boy (new entity)
This is the biggest new piece. Minimum fields:
- Name, phone, alternate phone
- Aadhaar / ID proof (photo upload — used for trust, not strict KYC at MVP)
- Vehicle type (bike / cycle / auto) + vehicle number
- Service area (list of pincodes or mandals they cover)
- Working hours, working days
- Live status: `available / on_delivery / offline`
- Current assigned order id (nullable)
- Bank/UPI for payouts
- Rating + total deliveries (for later)

We also need a way for them to log in. Since we already have phone-based auth for consumers, the cheapest path is the same phone + OTP/password pattern for riders.

## 2. End-to-end flow (proposed)

```
Consumer browses farmer page
   ↓
Adds items to cart → checkout
   ↓
Picks: [Self pickup] or [Home delivery]
   ↓ (if delivery)
Enters / confirms delivery address + slot
   ↓
Sees: items total + delivery fee = grand total
   ↓
Pays (UPI screenshot) OR chooses COD
   ↓
Order created with status = PENDING, delivery_status = UNASSIGNED
   ↓
Farmer accepts order (existing flow)
   ↓
Order becomes READY_FOR_PICKUP
   ↓
System (or manual admin step) finds a delivery boy whose
service area covers BOTH farmer pincode and consumer pincode,
and who is `available`. Sends them an alert.
   ↓
Rider accepts → status = ASSIGNED, rider sees pickup + drop card
   ↓
Rider marks PICKED_UP (with optional photo of parcel)
   ↓
Rider marks OUT_FOR_DELIVERY
   ↓
Rider marks DELIVERED (with OTP from consumer OR photo proof)
   ↓
Farmer + consumer both notified, payout queued
```

Failure branches we must design for up front, not later:
- Farmer declines → refund / cancel (already partly built)
- No rider available in X minutes → fall back to "pickup only" or queue
- Rider picks up but can't deliver (consumer unreachable) → return-to-farmer path
- COD: rider must collect cash and we track `cash_collected = true`

## 3. Delivery fee — decide before building
Options, simplest first:
1. **Flat fee per order** (e.g. ₹40) — easiest, ship in a day.
2. **Distance-based** (₹X base + ₹Y per km) — needs lat/lng on both ends + a distance calc.
3. **Weight + distance** — most accurate, most complex.

For MVP I'd push for **option 1 (flat) per pincode pair**, e.g. ₹30 same-pincode, ₹60 cross-pincode. Distance/weight can come later.

## 4. Data model additions (high level)
- `delivery_boys` — new table
- `orders` — add `delivery_type`, `delivery_status`, `delivery_address_*`, `delivery_slot`, `delivery_fee`, `delivery_boy_id`, `picked_up_at`, `delivered_at`, `delivery_otp`
- `consumer_addresses` — new table (so consumers can save multiple addresses)
- New SQL migration file in `/scripts`

## 5. What I need decided from you before I write code

1. **MVP scope** — is the delivery boy onboarded manually by you (you create their account), or do they self-register through a `/rider` page?
2. **Delivery fee model** — flat, pincode-pair, or distance? (I recommend pincode-pair flat for MVP.)
3. **Assignment** — manual (you pick the rider) or auto (first available in service area)? Manual is much faster to ship.
4. **Proof of delivery** — OTP shared with consumer, photo of handover, or both?
5. **Payment to rider** — does rider collect from consumer in COD, or do you settle weekly?
6. **Coverage area at launch** — one mandal/pincode, or all of AP? Defines how complex service-area matching needs to be.

Once you answer those six, I will draft a concrete plan (DB migration, screens, APIs, status transitions) and we can build it in phases — rider table + manual assignment first, then consumer-side delivery selection, then rider app screens.

> DEVELOPER

so suggest 3 important questions to ask for client .

> AGENT

Out of the six, these three decide ~80% of the scope. The other three can be answered later without rework.

### 1. Coverage area + delivery fee at launch
> "At launch, which areas will we deliver to — one mandal/town, a few nearby pincodes, or all of AP? And do you want a flat fee (e.g. ₹40 per order), a pincode-pair fee (₹30 same area, ₹60 outside), or distance-based pricing?"

**Why this is #1:** A single mandal with a flat fee = no maps, no distance math, simple DB. "All of AP with distance pricing" = lat/lng, Google Maps API, completely different build. This one answer changes the entire shape of the project.

### 2. Rider assignment — manual or automatic
> "When an order is ready, do you (or someone on your team) want to pick which delivery boy gets it, or should the system auto-assign to the first available rider in that area?"

**Why this is #2:** Manual assignment = we just need a small admin screen for you. Automatic assignment = we need a real-time "available riders" engine, a rider-side accept/reject screen, timeouts, fallbacks. Manual is ~3 days of work; auto is ~2 weeks.

### 3. Rider onboarding — you add them or they self-register
> "Will you personally onboard each delivery boy (collect their docs, create their account), or do you want a public 'Become a delivery partner' signup page where they register themselves and wait for approval?"

**Why this is #3:** "You add them" means no rider signup flow, no document upload, no approval queue — just a simple internal form. "Self-register" means a full onboarding screen, KYC upload to Supabase Storage, approval/rejection states, email/SMS notifications. Big difference in scope.

---

**My recommendation to push if the client is unsure:** start with **one mandal, flat fee, manual assignment, you onboard riders yourself**. Ship in days, learn from real orders, then expand. The remaining three questions (proof of delivery, rider payouts, weight/fragile flags) can be decided once 5–10 real deliveries have happened.

> DEVELOPER

can you suggest any simple method of delivery .

> AGENT

Yes — here's the dead-simplest version that can ship in **about a week** and still work for real customers.

## "WhatsApp-coordinated manual delivery"

The key idea: **at MVP, the delivery boy doesn't need an app, a login, or a dashboard. You (the owner) coordinate with them over WhatsApp.** All the "smart" parts come later.

### How it works end-to-end

1. **Consumer at checkout** picks `Home delivery` and enters address + landmark + alt phone. They see one flat fee (e.g. ₹40) added to the total.

2. **Consumer pays** (UPI screenshot, same as today) or chooses COD.

3. **Farmer accepts** the order in their existing dashboard. Status → `READY_FOR_PICKUP`.

4. **You (owner) get a WhatsApp/SMS alert** with: farmer pickup address, consumer drop address, items, payment status (paid/COD).

5. **You forward that message** to your delivery boy on WhatsApp. He says "ok bhai".

6. **He picks up**, you click one button in a tiny owner-only page: `Mark out for delivery`.

7. **He delivers**, takes a photo of the handover OR collects a 4-digit OTP that you SMS to the consumer. You click `Mark delivered`. Done.

### Why this is the right MVP

- **No rider app, no rider login, no admin panel.** Just one new page for you, hidden behind a password.
- **No maps, no distance, no auto-assignment.** Flat fee. You decide who delivers.
- **One trusted person** (a relative, a friend, anyone with a bike) handles all deliveries at launch. Pay them weekly in cash.
- **Reuses everything you already have**: orders table, UPI screenshot flow, farmer accept/decline, consumer phone auth.

### What we actually have to build

1. **One SQL migration** — add to `orders`: `delivery_type`, `delivery_status`, `delivery_address`, `delivery_landmark`, `delivery_otp`, `delivery_fee`, `delivered_at`.
2. **Checkout addition** — radio button "Pickup myself / Home delivery", address fields, fee shown.
3. **Farmer dashboard** — no change. They just see "Delivery: yes" on the order card.
4. **Owner-only page** at `/admin/deliveries` (basic password gate) showing every order with delivery, with three buttons per row: `Picked up`, `Out for delivery`, `Delivered`.
5. **WhatsApp link** — when an order is ready, a `wa.me` link pre-filled with pickup + drop details so you can forward it to your rider in one tap. No Twilio needed yet.

### What we deliberately skip at MVP

- Rider login / rider screen
- Auto-assignment by location
- Real-time tracking
- Distance-based pricing
- Multiple riders / service areas
- Rider KYC, ratings, payouts in-app

Each of those becomes a small upgrade later — and the data model above doesn't need to change to add them. We just add a `delivery_boy_id` column when you're ready to onboard a second rider.

---

**Bottom line:** ship this lean version, run 20–50 real deliveries, then let real problems (not imagined ones) decide what to build next. Want me to draft the SQL migration and the checkout changes for this version?

Can we do all these in our web app? Because the client asked not to involve WhatsApp for main functionalities. WhatsApp should only be used for side notifications. So we need a delivery dashboard in the web app itself.

Please suggest a simple and best method for now. It’s not a large-scale project — currently it’s only for a mandal, village, or small city.

> AGENT

Got it — fully in-app, WhatsApp only for "your order is on the way" style alerts. Here's the leanest version that still works as a real product for a single mandal.

## The model: 3 tiny dashboards, no coordination outside the app

Each actor logs in and sees exactly what they need. No WhatsApp in the operational loop.

```
Consumer  →  picks "delivery" at checkout, enters address, pays
                            ↓
Farmer    →  accepts order (existing flow), sees "Delivery: Yes" tag
                            ↓
Rider     →  logs in to /rider, sees a queue of orders ready for pickup,
             taps "Accept" → "Picked up" → "Delivered"
```

That's it. No owner-in-the-middle. No assignment screen. The rider's dashboard *is* the assignment system.

## Why this is the simplest in-app version

For one mandal with 1–3 riders, **you don't need an assignment engine**. You just need an open queue:

- Any logged-in rider sees all unassigned delivery orders.
- First rider to tap "Accept" owns it. The order disappears from everyone else's queue.
- After that, only that rider sees the order in their "My deliveries" list.

This is the same pattern Swiggy/Zomato used in their earliest days, and it removes the entire admin/assignment layer.

If you'd rather keep control at launch (only one trusted rider, you want to vet each delivery), there's an even simpler variant: **single rider, auto-assigned**. Every delivery order goes straight to that one rider's screen. No accept step. We literally hardcode `delivery_boy_id` until you onboard a second rider.

My recommendation: **start with the single-rider auto-assigned version**, switch to the open queue when you onboard rider #2. Same DB, one extra UI step.

## What we actually build

### 1. SQL migration (one file, ~10 columns)
Add to `orders`:
`delivery_type`, `delivery_status`, `delivery_address`, `delivery_landmark`, `delivery_pincode`, `delivery_fee`, `delivery_boy_id`, `picked_up_at`, `delivered_at`, `delivery_otp`.

New table `delivery_boys`: id, name, phone, password_hash, vehicle_number, active.

### 2. Consumer side (~1 day)
- Radio at checkout: `Pickup myself / Home delivery`
- Address fields + landmark + alt phone (saved to consumer profile so they reuse it)
- Flat fee added to total (e.g. ₹40, configurable per mandal)
- Order page shows live status: `Placed → Accepted → Picked up → Out for delivery → Delivered`

### 3. Rider side, route `/rider` (~1.5 days)
- Login: phone + password (mirror of consumer auth, we already have the code)
- One screen, mobile-first:
  - **"Available pickups"** — orders to accept
  - **"My deliveries"** — orders in progress
- Per-order card: pickup name+address+phone (tap to call), drop name+address+phone (tap to call), item list, COD amount if any, three buttons: `Picked up` → `Out for delivery` → `Delivered`
- Final tap shows an OTP input; consumer reads OTP off their order page, rider types it in → delivered.

### 4. Owner view, route `/admin/deliveries` (~0.5 day)
- Plain table of every delivery order, current status, which rider, timestamps.
- One button: `Reassign` (in case a rider is sick / phone died).
- Password-gated for now, no full admin panel.

### 5. WhatsApp notifications — side channel only
- `wa.me` deep links from the order page so the **consumer** can ping the rider, or the rider can ping the consumer ("I'm at your gate"). One-tap, no API.
- No Twilio needed at MVP. When you're ready, swap the deep links for Twilio outgoing alerts — same trigger points (`accepted`, `picked_up`, `delivered`).

## Total scope estimate
About **4–5 working days**, single developer. No maps, no distance math, no admin panel, no rider app. Everything inside the existing Next.js app.

## What you need to decide to unblock me
Just two things now:

1. **Single-rider auto-assign or open queue?** (I recommend single-rider for week 1, swap to open queue when rider #2 joins.)
2. **Flat delivery fee at launch?** (Pick any number — ₹30, ₹40, ₹50. Easy to change later.)

Say the word and I'll draft the SQL migration + rider login + rider dashboard as the first PR.

okay explain in easy way how it works wiithout technical terms

> AGENT

Sure — here it is in plain words, like telling a friend.

## Imagine three people using their phones

**The customer (buyer)**
**The farmer (seller)**
**The delivery boy**

Each one opens the same website on their phone and logs in. They each see only what they need to see.

---

### Step 1 — Customer places an order
The customer opens the website, picks her vegetables from a farmer's page, and goes to checkout. She sees two choices:

- "I will come and pick it up myself"
- "Please deliver to my home" *(adds ₹40)*

She picks home delivery, types her house address and a landmark ("near the temple"), and pays — same as today.

### Step 2 — Farmer gets the order
The farmer opens his page, sees the new order with a small tag: **"Home Delivery"**. He prepares the vegetables and taps "Order Ready", just like he does now.

### Step 3 — Delivery boy sees the job
The delivery boy opens his own page on his phone. He sees a list:

> **New pickup**
> Pick up from: Ramesh (farmer) — Anand Nagar
> Deliver to: Lakshmi — 4th Street, near temple
> Items: 2 kg tomatoes, 1 kg onions
> Cash to collect: ₹0 (already paid)
>
> *[Accept this delivery]*

He taps **Accept**. The job is now his. No one else sees it.

### Step 4 — He picks it up
He rides to the farmer's place, collects the bag. On his phone he taps **"Picked up"**.

At this moment, the customer's phone shows: *"Your order is on the way!"* (and we can also send a WhatsApp message if you want — but only as a friendly reminder, not as the main system).

### Step 5 — He delivers it
He reaches Lakshmi's house, hands over the vegetables. Lakshmi opens her order page and reads out a small 4-digit code shown on her screen. The delivery boy types that code into his phone and taps **"Delivered"**.

Done. The order is closed. Everyone's screen updates.

---

### What the owner (you) sees
You have your own page where you can see **every order and where it is right now**:

- Lakshmi's order — *picked up, 10 mins ago*
- Suresh's order — *waiting for delivery boy*
- Ravi's order — *delivered*

If something goes wrong (delivery boy's phone is off, he's sick), you can tap one button to give the order to someone else.

---

### Why this is easy

- **No phone calls needed.** Everyone just taps buttons on their phone.
- **No WhatsApp coordination.** WhatsApp is only used for friendly "your order is coming" messages, not to run the business.
- **No paperwork.** Every step is saved automatically.
- **The customer always knows where her order is.** Like Swiggy, but simple.
- **The delivery boy doesn't need to download anything.** He just opens the website on his phone, like the farmer does today.

---

### Real-life example in one line

> *Lakshmi orders 2 kg tomatoes → Ramesh (farmer) accepts → Krishna (delivery boy) taps "Accept" → picks up → drops at Lakshmi's house → types the code she shows him → done.*

That's the whole flow. Everything happens inside our website. No app to install for anyone.

> DEVELOPER

but farmer , consumer and delivery boy need each other phone number and contact details right for security and delivry query

> AGENT

Yes — very good question. Let me explain in plain words how each person sees the right contact at the right time, without showing everyone everything.

## The simple rule: "Need to know, at the right time"

Think of it like a courier slip. The courier boy sees the sender and receiver names + phone numbers on the slip. The sender doesn't get the receiver's full phone book. Same idea.

---

### Who sees whose number, and when

**While the order is in progress:**

| Person | Sees on their screen | Why |
|---|---|---|
| **Farmer** | Delivery boy's name + phone | To call him if he hasn't reached for pickup |
| **Delivery boy** | Farmer's name + phone *(for pickup)* AND customer's name + phone *(for drop)* | He needs both — to find the farmer, and to call customer at the gate |
| **Customer** | Delivery boy's name + phone | To call him if he's delayed or can't find the house |
| **You (owner)** | Everyone's number, all the time | You're the safety net for any complaint |

So:
- Farmer **does not** see the customer's number, because they don't need to talk.
- Customer **does not** see the farmer's number, because the delivery boy is the link.
- If something is wrong (bad tomatoes, missing item), the customer raises a complaint **inside the website** — and you can call both sides.

---

### How it shows up on the phone

On each screen, the contact looks like this:

> **Pickup from:** Ramesh (Farmer)
> Anand Nagar, near water tank
> 📞 **Call Ramesh** *(big green button)*

One tap — the phone dials. No copying, no saving needed. But if he wants to save the number, he can.

---

### After the delivery is complete

Once the order is marked **Delivered**, we can do one of two things — your choice:

1. **Keep numbers visible forever** in the order history. Simple. (Recommended for now — easy to handle disputes a week later.)
2. **Auto-hide the numbers after 7 days.** Better privacy. We can add this whenever you want.

---

### Extra protection for the future (not needed now)

Later, when you're bigger, we can do what Swiggy/Ola/Dunzo do — **masked numbers**:

- Customer sees a fake number like `080-xxxxxx`.
- She calls it; it secretly forwards to the delivery boy.
- After the order is done, the fake number stops working.

This needs a paid service (Twilio / Exotel ~₹1–2 per call). **Not needed for one mandal at launch.** We can add it the day you want.

---

### What about safety / security worries?

A few simple safeguards inside the website itself:

1. **Every call button click is logged.** If someone misuses a number, we know who looked it up and when.
2. **Delivery boy's ID proof is on file** (Aadhaar photo uploaded once during signup). The customer can see "Verified ✓" on the rider's profile.
3. **The 4-digit OTP at delivery** proves the right person received the goods. No OTP = not delivered = no payment to rider.
4. **Inside the order page**, both customer and farmer can tap "Report a problem" — comes straight to you, with all three numbers attached, so you can sort it out.

---

### One-line summary

> Each person sees only the phone number of the person they need to talk to *right now*, with a one-tap call button. You see everything, all the time, so you can handle any problem.

That's enough for a mandal-level launch. Nothing fancy, but safe and practical.

> DEVELOPER

how delivery boy geti delivers nearby or anything else

> AGENT

Good question — let me explain how the delivery boy actually **finds and picks up nearby orders**. For a small mandal, this is much simpler than you might think.

## The truth about a small mandal

In a single mandal or village, **everything is already nearby**. Pickup and drop are usually 1–5 km apart. So at the start, the delivery boy doesn't really need maps or GPS — he just opens his screen and sees all the orders waiting.

But as you grow (multiple villages, 2–3 riders), we add small filters so each rider sees only what's relevant to him. Let me show you the three stages.

---

## Stage 1 — One rider, one mandal *(this is where you start)*

Krishna is your only delivery boy. He covers the whole mandal.

He logs into his page and sees:

> **Orders waiting for pickup**
>
> 🟢 **Lakshmi → Anand Nagar**
> Pick from Ramesh (farmer) — 1.2 km away
> Drop at Lakshmi's house — 800 m from farmer
> Items: 3 kg veggies | Already paid
> *[Accept]*
>
> 🟢 **Suresh → Gandhi Street**
> Pick from Venkat (farmer) — 2 km away
> Drop at Suresh's house — 1 km from farmer
> Items: 5 kg rice | COD ₹450
> *[Accept]*

He looks at the list, picks whichever one he wants, taps **Accept**, and goes. **No matching, no maps needed.**

Distance numbers are optional at this stage — he already knows the village. We can just show the area name ("Anand Nagar", "Gandhi Street") and that's enough.

---

## Stage 2 — Two or three riders covering different areas

Now you have:
- Krishna covers **Anand Nagar + Gandhi Street**
- Suresh covers **Market Road + Old Town**

When each rider signs up, you (or he) ticks the areas he covers — like a checklist:

> *Which areas can you deliver to?*
> ☑ Anand Nagar
> ☑ Gandhi Street
> ☐ Market Road
> ☐ Old Town

After that, **the website automatically shows each rider only the orders in his areas**. Krishna never sees Old Town orders. Suresh never sees Anand Nagar orders. No confusion, no overlap.

If two riders cover the same area, both see it — first one to tap "Accept" gets it. Like first-come, first-served.

---

## Stage 3 — Many riders, large town *(later, only if you grow big)*

This is the Swiggy / Dunzo style. Each rider's phone shares GPS location. The website shows him the **closest** orders first, with real distance:

> 🟢 Lakshmi's order — *400 m from you*
> 🟢 Suresh's order — *1.2 km from you*
> 🟢 Anita's order — *3 km from you*

This needs:
- The rider's permission to share location (one-time popup on his phone)
- A maps service (Google or OpenStreetMap)
- A bit more code

**You don't need this for one mandal.** We can add it the day you go beyond 5–6 riders or expand to a city.

---

## What about "the order is on the other side of town"?

Two simple safety nets:

1. **The rider can refuse.** If he sees an order and doesn't want it, he just doesn't tap Accept. Someone else picks it up, or you assign it manually.

2. **You can manually assign**, from your owner page. If Lakshmi has been waiting 20 minutes and no rider has accepted, you tap one button: *"Assign to Krishna"*. Order goes straight to his screen. He gets a small alert.

---

## How he knows a new order arrived

Three options, in order of simplicity:

1. **He keeps the page open** — orders appear automatically every few seconds. Works fine if he's actively looking for jobs. (Easiest. Free.)

2. **Browser notification** — like Gmail. When a new order comes in, his phone makes a small "ding" even if the page is in the background. Free, just needs his one-time permission.

3. **WhatsApp ping** — for big orders or after long silence, the system sends him a WhatsApp message: *"New order in Anand Nagar — open your page."* Only as a friendly nudge, not as the main system. (Costs a tiny amount per message via Twilio later.)

I'd recommend **#1 and #2 together for now** — both are free, and they're enough.

---

## Putting it all together — for your scale today

> Krishna opens the website on his phone in the morning.
> He sees a list of 3 pending pickups in the mandal.
> He picks the one closest to him, taps Accept, and goes.
> When new orders come in during the day, his phone goes *ding*.
> He never sees orders outside the mandal.
> If he's busy and doesn't accept, you can tap to push it to him directly.

That's it. No GPS, no maps, no fancy matching — and it works perfectly for a single mandal with 1–3 riders.

When you grow, we just **switch on** the area-checklist (Stage 2) or the distance feature (Stage 3) — no need to rebuild anything.

Yes, we can do both — but I want to make sure I understand what you mean, because "delivery code" can mean two different things. Let me lay out the two possibilities:

---

### Possibility A — Delivery code = the *order handover code*

This is what I explained earlier. It has nothing to do with login.

**How login works:**
- The delivery boy signs up himself on the website (name, phone, vehicle number, photo of ID).
- You see his application on your owner page → tap **Approve**.
- After approval, he logs in any time with **his phone number + password** he set himself.

**Then, separately, for each delivery:**
- When he reaches the customer's house, he asks for the **4-digit code**.
- The customer reads it off her phone screen.
- He types it in → "Delivered" → done.

So the **login** is his own, and the **delivery code** is a per-order confirmation code that proves he actually met the customer.

---

### Possibility B — Delivery code = an *onboarding/approval code*

This is a different idea. Here, the code is for getting into the system the first time.

**How it works:**
- A new rider signs up himself (name, phone, vehicle, ID photo).
- You decide if you trust him. If yes, you generate a **secret code** in your owner page — say, `YFF-K2847`.
- You give him this code in person (or over a phone call).
- He enters this code once on his login page — only then his account is **activated**.
- After that, he logs in normally with phone + password.

This way, **no random person can become a delivery boy** even if they sign up online. They need your code to actually start working. Like a shop giving you a uniform — you can apply, but you can't start working without it.

---

### My recommendation — do **both**, because they solve different problems

Here's the full picture, combining everything:

1. **Rider signs up himself** on `/rider/signup` — fills in his name, phone, vehicle, uploads ID photo, sets his password.
2. **You approve him** in your owner page. *(Optional: you also give him an activation code for extra safety.)*
3. **He logs in daily** with his phone + password (his own login).
4. **For every order he delivers**, he collects the **4-digit handover code** from the customer.

This gives you:
- ✅ Self-service signup (you don't have to type each rider's details)
- ✅ Your approval gate (no random person can deliver)
- ✅ His own daily login (he controls his account)
- ✅ Per-order safety (proof he actually met the customer)

---

**Tell me which one you want and I'll start building:**

1. Option A only (his own login + per-order handover code) — *simplest*
2. Option B only (signup needs your approval code to activate)
3. **Both A and B together** — *most complete, my recommendation*

Once you pick, I'll start writing the code straight away.

```
import { createHmac, timingSafeEqual } from 'crypto'
import type { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'

// HMAC-signed session token (server-only). Stored in an HTTP-only cookie so
// XSS cannot exfiltrate it. Format: <consumerId>.<issuedAtMs>.<hmacHexBase64url>
//
// We never put consumer name or phone in the token — those live in the DB.
// localStorage holds the public profile copy used purely for UI display, and
// the server re-derives identity from the cookie on every request.

const COOKIE_NAME = 'yff_consumer'
const TOKEN_TTL_MS = 30 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000 // 30 days

export function getSessionSecret(): string {
  const secret = process.env.SESSION_SECRET
  if (!secret || secret.length < 32) {
    throw new Error(
      'SESSION_SECRET env var is missing or too short. Set a 32+ char random string in your environment (e.g. `openssl rand -hex 32`).',
    )
  }
  return secret
}

function b64url(buf: Buffer): string {
  return buf.toString('base64').replace(/\+/g, '-').replace(/\//g, '_').replace(/=+$/, '')
}

function sign(payload: string): string {
  const secret=[REDACTED]()
  return b64url(createHmac('sha256', secret).update(payload).digest())
}

export function createSessionToken(consumerId: string): string {
  const issuedAt = Date.now()
  const payload = `${consumerId}.${issuedAt}`
  const sig = sign(payload)
  return `${payload}.${sig}`
}

export type SessionPayload = { consumerId: string; issuedAt: number }

export function verifySessionToken(token: string | undefined | null): SessionPayload | null {
  if (!token) return null
  const parts = token.split('.')
  if (parts.length !== 3) return null
  const [consumerId, issuedAtStr, sig] = parts
  const issuedAt = Number(issuedAtStr)
  if (!consumerId || !Number.isFinite(issuedAt)) return null
  if (Date.now() - issuedAt > TOKEN_TTL_MS) return null

  let expected: string
  try {
    expected = sign(`${consumerId}.${issuedAtStr}`)
  } catch {
    return null
  }
  const a = Buffer.from(sig)
  const b = Buffer.from(expected)
  if (a.length !== b.length) return null
  if (!timingSafeEqual(a, b)) return null
  return { consumerId, issuedAt }
}

export function getConsumerSessionFromRequest(req: NextRequest): SessionPayload | null {
  const token = req.cookies.get(COOKIE_NAME)?.value
  return verifySessionToken(token)
}

export function setSessionCookie(res: NextResponse, consumerId: string): void {
  const token=[REDACTED](consumerId)
  res.cookies.set(COOKIE_NAME, token, {
    httpOnly: true,
    secure: process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production',
    sameSite: 'lax',
    path: '/',
    maxAge: Math.floor(TOKEN_TTL_MS / 1000),
  })
}

export function clearSessionCookie(res: NextResponse): void {
  res.cookies.set(COOKIE_NAME, '', {
    httpOnly: true,
    secure: process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production',
    sameSite: 'lax',
    path: '/',
    maxAge: 0,
  })
}

export const CONSUMER_SESSION_COOKIE_NAME = COOKIE_NAME
import { scryptSync, randomBytes, timingSafeEqual } from 'crypto'

// scrypt with per-user salt — matches the existing farmer login pattern
// Format on disk: "salt_hex:hash_hex"
export function hashPassword(password: string): string {
  const salt = randomBytes(16).toString('hex')
  const hash = scryptSync(password, salt, 64).toString('hex')
  return `${salt}:${hash}`
}

export function verifyPassword(password: string, stored: string): boolean {
  try {
    const [salt, hash] = stored.split(':')
    if (!salt || !hash) return false
    const hashBuffer = Buffer.from(hash, 'hex')
    const derived = scryptSync(password, salt, 64)
    if (hashBuffer.length !== derived.length) return false
    return timingSafeEqual(hashBuffer, derived)
  } catch {
    return false
  }
}
// Normalize an Indian phone number to its 10 trailing digits.
// Strips +91 / 91 / 0 prefixes and any non-digit characters. Returns '' when invalid.
export function normalizePhone(raw: string | null | undefined): string {
  if (!raw) return ''
  const digits = String(raw).replace(/\D/g, '').slice(-10)
  return digits.length === 10 ? digits : ''
}
// Tiny in-memory token-bucket-ish limiter. Per-process only — fine for a single
// Vercel region; for multi-region scale we'd swap to Redis. Enough to slow
// brute-force on /api/consumer/login until that's needed.

type Bucket = { count: number; windowStart: number }
const buckets = new Map<string, Bucket>()

const SWEEP_INTERVAL_MS = 60 * 60 * 1000 // 1h
let lastSweep = Date.now()

function sweep(now: number) {
  if (now - lastSweep < SWEEP_INTERVAL_MS) return
  lastSweep = now
  // Drop entries whose window has long expired (≥1h)
  for (const [k, b] of buckets) {
    if (now - b.windowStart > SWEEP_INTERVAL_MS) buckets.delete(k)
  }
}

/**
 * Returns true when the request is allowed (under the limit), false when blocked.
 * @param key identifier — combine ip + phone for login attempts
 * @param max max attempts per window
 * @param windowMs window length in ms
 */
export function rateLimit(key: string, max: number, windowMs: number): boolean {
  const now = Date.now()
  sweep(now)
  const bucket = buckets.get(key)
  if (!bucket || now - bucket.windowStart > windowMs) {
    buckets.set(key, { count: 1, windowStart: now })
    return true
  }
  if (bucket.count >= max) return false
  bucket.count += 1
  return true
}
```

```
-- ================================================================
-- YFF — Consumer authentication
-- Run in: Supabase Dashboard → SQL Editor → New Query → Run
-- ================================================================

-- 1. Consumer accounts table
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS consumers_auth (
  id             uuid         PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  name           text,
  phone          varchar(15)  UNIQUE NOT NULL,
  password_hash  text         NOT NULL,
  created_at     timestamptz  DEFAULT now(),
  last_login_at  timestamptz
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_consumers_auth_phone ON consumers_auth(phone);

-- 2. RLS — only the service-role key (used in /api/consumer/* handlers) can
-- read or write. The anon key used by the browser cannot touch this table.
ALTER TABLE consumers_auth ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;

-- No policies = no anon access. Service role bypasses RLS automatically.

-- 3. Sanity check helper for ops debugging
COMMENT ON TABLE consumers_auth IS
  'Consumer accounts. Server-only access via service role; passwords scrypt-hashed.';
-- ================================================================
-- YFF — Link orders to authenticated consumer accounts
-- Run AFTER consumer-auth-migration.sql
-- ================================================================

-- 1. Foreign key — orders placed by registered consumers carry their account id.
--    Older anonymous orders keep consumer_id = NULL. Account deletion sets to NULL
--    so we never lose the order history a farmer needs.
ALTER TABLE orders
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS consumer_id uuid REFERENCES consumers_auth(id) ON DELETE SET NULL;

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_orders_consumer_id ON orders(consumer_id);
-- ================================================================
-- YFF — Payment screenshot upload (mandatory for UPI orders)
-- Run AFTER orders-consumer-id-migration.sql
-- ================================================================

-- Object path inside the private 'payment-proofs' storage bucket. Never store
-- a public URL — clients fetch a signed URL via /api/orders/[id]/proof.
ALTER TABLE orders
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS payment_proof_path text;

-- Private bucket. The anon key has no policies on it, so only API routes
-- (using the service role) can upload, list, or sign URLs.
INSERT INTO storage.buckets (id, name, public)
VALUES ('payment-proofs', 'payment-proofs', false)
ON CONFLICT (id) DO NOTHING;
-- ================================================================
-- Decline Reason Migration
-- Run in: Supabase Dashboard → SQL Editor → New Query → Run
--
-- Adds a mandatory reason text when a farmer declines an order.
-- The reason is shown to the consumer on their order page.
-- ================================================================

ALTER TABLE orders ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS decline_reason text;
-- ================================================================
-- YFF — Farmer COD toggle
-- Run in: Supabase Dashboard → SQL Editor → New Query → Run
-- ================================================================

-- Per-farmer flag: when true, buyers can choose Cash on Delivery in checkout.
-- Default OFF — farmer must explicitly opt in from dashboard settings.
ALTER TABLE farmers
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS cod_enabled boolean DEFAULT false;

-- Backfill existing rows so NULL doesn't leak through to clients
UPDATE farmers SET cod_enabled = false WHERE cod_enabled IS NULL;
```

```
import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'
import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'
import { hashPassword } from '@/lib/password'
import { normalizePhone } from '@/lib/phone'
import { setSessionCookie } from '@/lib/session'
import { rateLimit } from '@/lib/rate-limit'

export const runtime = 'nodejs'
export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'

export async function POST(req: NextRequest) {
  // Lightweight anti-abuse: 10 registrations per IP per hour
  const ip = req.headers.get('x-forwarded-for')?.split(',')[0]?.trim() || 'unknown'
  if (!rateLimit(`reg:${ip}`, 10, 60 * 60 * 1000)) {
    return NextResponse.json(
      { error: 'Too many sign-up attempts. Try again in an hour.' },
      { status: 429 },
    )
  }

  const body = await req.json().catch(() => null)
  if (!body || typeof body !== 'object') {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Invalid request.' }, { status: 400 })
  }

  const name = String(body.name ?? '').trim().slice(0, 80)
  const phone = normalizePhone(body.phone)
  const password = String(body.password ?? '')

  if (!name) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Please enter your name.' }, { status: 400 })
  }
  if (!phone) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Enter a valid 10-digit phone number.' }, { status: 400 })
  }
  if (password.length < 6) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Password must be at least 6 characters.' }, { status: 400 })
  }
  if (password.length > 128) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Password is too long.' }, { status: 400 })
  }

  const supabase = createClient(
    process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!,
    process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY!,
  )

  console.log('[YFF register] attempt phone=%s name=%s', phone, name)

  // Reject duplicate phone (UNIQUE constraint also enforces, but check first
  // to give a clean error message)
  const { data: existing, error: lookupErr } = await supabase
    .from('consumers_auth')
    .select('id')
    .eq('phone', phone)
    .maybeSingle()

  if (lookupErr) {
    console.error('[YFF register] lookup failed:', lookupErr.code, lookupErr.message)
    if (
      lookupErr.message?.includes('does not exist')
      || lookupErr.code === '42P01'
    ) {
      return NextResponse.json(
        { error: 'consumers_auth table is missing. Run scripts/consumer-auth-migration.sql in Supabase first.' },
        { status: 500 },
      )
    }
    return NextResponse.json(
      { error: `Database error: ${lookupErr.message}` },
      { status: 500 },
    )
  }

  if (existing) {
    console.log('[YFF register] phone already registered, id=%s', existing.id)
    return NextResponse.json(
      { error: 'An account already exists for this phone. Please log in instead.' },
      { status: 409 },
    )
  }

  const password_hash = hashPassword(password)
  const { data: created, error: insertErr } = await supabase
    .from('consumers_auth')
    .insert({ name, phone, password_hash })
    .select('id, name, phone')
    .single()

  if (insertErr || !created) {
    console.error('[YFF register] insert failed:', insertErr?.code, insertErr?.message)
    // Surface the underlying error so we can debug instead of guessing
    return NextResponse.json(
      { error: insertErr?.message || 'Could not create account. Please try again.' },
      { status: 500 },
    )
  }

  console.log('[YFF register] success id=%s', created.id)

  const res = NextResponse.json({
    ok: true,
    consumer: { id: created.id, name: created.name, phone: created.phone },
  })
  try {
    setSessionCookie(res, created.id)
  } catch (e) {
    const msg = e instanceof Error ? e.message : 'Session setup failed.'
    console.error('[YFF register] setSessionCookie failed:', msg)
    return NextResponse.json({ error: msg }, { status: 500 })
  }
  return res
}
import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'
import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'
import { verifyPassword } from '@/lib/password'
import { normalizePhone } from '@/lib/phone'
import { setSessionCookie } from '@/lib/session'
import { rateLimit } from '@/lib/rate-limit'

export const runtime = 'nodejs'
export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'

export async function POST(req: NextRequest) {
  const body = await req.json().catch(() => null)
  if (!body || typeof body !== 'object') {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Invalid request.' }, { status: 400 })
  }

  const phone = normalizePhone(body.phone)
  const password = String(body.password ?? '')

  if (!phone) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Enter a valid 10-digit phone number.' }, { status: 400 })
  }
  if (!password) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Enter your password.' }, { status: 400 })
  }

  // Brute-force throttle: 5 login attempts per phone per 10 min, plus 30 / IP
  const ip = req.headers.get('x-forwarded-for')?.split(',')[0]?.trim() || 'unknown'
  if (
    !rateLimit(`login:phone:${phone}`, 5, 10 * 60 * 1000) ||
    !rateLimit(`login:ip:${ip}`, 30, 10 * 60 * 1000)
  ) {
    return NextResponse.json(
      { error: 'Too many login attempts. Please try again in a few minutes.' },
      { status: 429 },
    )
  }

  const supabase = createClient(
    process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!,
    process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY!,
  )

  console.log('[YFF login] attempt phone=%s', phone)

  const { data: user, error: lookupErr } = await supabase
    .from('consumers_auth')
    .select('id, name, phone, password_hash')
    .eq('phone', phone)
    .maybeSingle()

  if (lookupErr) {
    console.error('[YFF login] lookup failed:', lookupErr.code, lookupErr.message)
    if (
      lookupErr.message?.includes('does not exist')
      || lookupErr.code === '42P01'
    ) {
      return NextResponse.json(
        { error: 'consumers_auth table is missing. Run scripts/consumer-auth-migration.sql in Supabase first.' },
        { status: 500 },
      )
    }
    return NextResponse.json(
      { error: `Database error: ${lookupErr.message}` },
      { status: 500 },
    )
  }

  if (!user) {
    console.log('[YFF login] no account for phone=%s', phone)
    return NextResponse.json(
      { error: 'No account found. Please sign up. / ఖాతా లేదు, సైన్ అప్ చేయండి' },
      { status: 404 },
    )
  }

  if (!user.password_hash || !verifyPassword(password, user.password_hash)) {
    console.log('[YFF login] wrong password phone=%s', phone)
    return NextResponse.json(
      { error: 'Wrong password / తప్పు పాస్‌వర్డ్' },
      { status: 401 },
    )
  }

  await supabase
    .from('consumers_auth')
    .update({ last_login_at: new Date().toISOString() })
    .eq('id', user.id)

  console.log('[YFF login] success id=%s', user.id)

  const res = NextResponse.json({
    ok: true,
    consumer: { id: user.id, name: user.name, phone: user.phone },
  })
  try {
    setSessionCookie(res, user.id)
  } catch (e) {
    const msg = e instanceof Error ? e.message : 'Session setup failed.'
    console.error('[YFF login] setSessionCookie failed:', msg)
    return NextResponse.json({ error: msg }, { status: 500 })
  }
  return res
}
import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'
import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'
import { getConsumerSessionFromRequest, clearSessionCookie } from '@/lib/session'

export const runtime = 'nodejs'
export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'

export async function GET(req: NextRequest) {
  const session = getConsumerSessionFromRequest(req)
  if (!session) {
    return NextResponse.json({ consumer: null }, { status: 200 })
  }

  const supabase = createClient(
    process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!,
    process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY!,
  )

  const { data: user } = await supabase
    .from('consumers_auth')
    .select('id, name, phone')
    .eq('id', session.consumerId)
    .maybeSingle()

  if (!user) {
    // Cookie references a deleted account — clear it so the client logs out
    const res = NextResponse.json({ consumer: null }, { status: 200 })
    clearSessionCookie(res)
    return res
  }

  return NextResponse.json({ consumer: user })
}
import { NextResponse } from 'next/server'
import { clearSessionCookie } from '@/lib/session'

export const runtime = 'nodejs'
export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'

export async function POST() {
  const res = NextResponse.json({ ok: true })
  clearSessionCookie(res)
  return res
}
```

```
import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'
import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'
import { getConsumerSessionFromRequest } from '@/lib/session'
import { getTierPrice } from '@/lib/pricing'

export const runtime = 'nodejs'
export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'

type IncomingItem = { listingId: string; qty: number }

type ListingRow = {
  id: string
  name: string
  unit: string | null
  stock_qty: number | null
  farmer_id: string
  price_tier_1_qty: number | null
  price_tier_1_price: number | null
  price_tier_2_qty: number | null
  price_tier_2_price: number | null
  price_tier_3_price: number | null
}

const UUID_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i

function bad(msg: string, status = 400) {
  return NextResponse.json({ error: msg }, { status })
}

export async function POST(req: NextRequest) {
  const session = getConsumerSessionFromRequest(req)
  if (!session) return bad('Please log in to place an order.', 401)

  const body = await req.json().catch(() => null) as
    | {
        farmerId?: string
        paymentMethod?: string
        pickupLocation?: string | null
        pickupDay?: string | null
        items?: IncomingItem[]
      }
    | null

  if (!body) return bad('Invalid request body.')
  // pickupDay is currently UI-only (not a DB column); accept and ignore.
  const { farmerId, paymentMethod, pickupLocation, items } = body

  if (!farmerId || !UUID_RE.test(farmerId)) return bad('Invalid farmer.')
  if (paymentMethod !== 'upi' && paymentMethod !== 'cod') return bad('Invalid payment method.')
  if (!Array.isArray(items) || items.length === 0) return bad('Cart is empty.')
  if (items.length > 50) return bad('Too many items.')
  for (const it of items) {
    if (!it || typeof it !== 'object') return bad('Invalid item.')
    if (!it.listingId || !UUID_RE.test(it.listingId)) return bad('Invalid listing id.')
    if (!Number.isFinite(it.qty) || it.qty <= 0 || it.qty > 10000) return bad('Invalid quantity.')
  }

  const supabase = createClient(
    process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!,
    process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY!,
  )

  // Authoritative consumer profile (we never trust the client for buyer name/phone)
  const { data: consumer } = await supabase
    .from('consumers_auth')
    .select('id, name, phone')
    .eq('id', session.consumerId)
    .maybeSingle()

  if (!consumer) return bad('Account not found. Please log in again.', 401)

  // Farmer COD acceptance check
  const { data: farmer } = await supabase
    .from('farmers')
    .select('id, cod_enabled')
    .eq('id', farmerId)
    .maybeSingle()

  if (!farmer) return bad('Farmer not found.', 404)
  if (paymentMethod === 'cod' && farmer.cod_enabled !== true) {
    return bad('This farmer is not accepting Cash on Delivery.')
  }

  // Pull live listing rows — never trust prices from the client cart
  const listingIds = items.map((i) => i.listingId)
  const { data: listings } = await supabase
    .from('produce_listings')
    .select(
      'id, name, unit, stock_qty, farmer_id, price_tier_1_qty, price_tier_1_price, price_tier_2_qty, price_tier_2_price, price_tier_3_price',
    )
    .in('id', listingIds) as { data: ListingRow[] | null }

  if (!listings || listings.length !== listingIds.length) {
    return bad('One or more items in your cart are no longer available.')
  }

  const listingById = new Map(listings.map((l) => [l.id, l]))
  const rows: Array<Record<string, unknown>> = []
  let total = 0

  for (const item of items) {
    const listing = listingById.get(item.listingId)
    if (!listing) return bad('Item missing.')
    if (listing.farmer_id !== farmerId) return bad('Items must belong to the same farmer.')
    if (listing.stock_qty != null && item.qty > listing.stock_qty) {
      return bad(`Only ${listing.stock_qty} ${listing.unit || 'kg'} of ${listing.name} available right now.`)
    }

    const unitPrice = getTierPrice(item.qty, {
      priceTier1Qty: listing.price_tier_1_qty,
      priceTier1Price: listing.price_tier_1_price,
      priceTier2Qty: listing.price_tier_2_qty,
      priceTier2Price: listing.price_tier_2_price,
      priceTier3Price: listing.price_tier_3_price,
    })

    const linePrice = unitPrice != null ? Math.round(unitPrice * item.qty) : null
    if (linePrice == null || linePrice <= 0) {
      return bad(`Price not set for ${listing.name}. Please ask the farmer.`)
    }
    total += linePrice

    rows.push({
      farmer_id: farmerId,
      produce_listing_id: listing.id,
      produce_name: listing.name,
      quantity: item.qty,
      unit: listing.unit || 'kg',
      total_price: linePrice,
      buyer_name: consumer.name || 'Buyer',
      buyer_phone: consumer.phone,
      consumer_id: consumer.id,
      pickup_location: typeof pickupLocation === 'string' ? pickupLocation.slice(0, 200) : null,
      status: 'pending',
      payment_method: paymentMethod,
      payment_status: 'pending',
    })
  }

  const { data: inserted, error: insertErr } = await supabase
    .from('orders')
    .insert(rows)
    .select('id')

  if (insertErr || !inserted) {
    console.error('[YFF] place-order insert failed:', insertErr?.message)
    return bad('Could not place order. Please try again.', 500)
  }

  return NextResponse.json({
    ok: true,
    orderIds: inserted.map((r) => r.id),
    total,
  })
}
import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'
import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'
import { getConsumerSessionFromRequest } from '@/lib/session'

export const runtime = 'nodejs'
export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'

const UUID_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i
const ALLOWED_TYPES = new Set(['image/jpeg', 'image/jpg', 'image/png', 'image/webp'])
const MAX_BYTES = 5 * 1024 * 1024 // 5MB

function bad(msg: string, status = 400) {
  return NextResponse.json({ error: msg }, { status })
}

export async function POST(req: NextRequest) {
  const session = getConsumerSessionFromRequest(req)
  if (!session) return bad('Please log in.', 401)

  let form: FormData
  try {
    form = await req.formData()
  } catch {
    return bad('Invalid upload payload.')
  }

  const orderIdsRaw = String(form.get('orderIds') ?? '')
  const file = form.get('file')

  // Comma-separated list — multi-line UPI orders share one proof
  const orderIds = orderIdsRaw.split(',').map((s) => s.trim()).filter(Boolean)
  if (orderIds.length === 0) return bad('Missing order ids.')
  if (orderIds.length > 50) return bad('Too many orders.')
  for (const id of orderIds) {
    if (!UUID_RE.test(id)) return bad('Invalid order id.')
  }
  if (!(file instanceof File)) return bad('No file attached.')
  if (!ALLOWED_TYPES.has(file.type)) return bad('Only JPG, PNG, or WEBP screenshots are allowed.')
  if (file.size === 0) return bad('Empty file.')
  if (file.size > MAX_BYTES) return bad('Screenshot is too large. Max 5MB.')

  const supabase = createClient(
    process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!,
    process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY!,
  )

  // Confirm every order belongs to this consumer — refuse the entire batch if not
  const { data: orders } = await supabase
    .from('orders')
    .select('id, consumer_id')
    .in('id', orderIds)

  if (!orders || orders.length !== orderIds.length) {
    return bad('Order not found.', 404)
  }
  if (orders.some((o) => o.consumer_id !== session.consumerId)) {
    return bad('Not your order.', 403)
  }

  const ext = file.type === 'image/png' ? 'png'
    : file.type === 'image/webp' ? 'webp'
    : 'jpg'
  const path = `${session.consumerId}/${orderIds[0]}-${Date.now()}.${ext}`

  const { error: upErr } = await supabase.storage
    .from('payment-proofs')
    .upload(path, file, { contentType: file.type, upsert: true })

  if (upErr) {
    console.error('[YFF] proof upload failed:', upErr.message)
    return bad('Could not save screenshot. Please try again.', 500)
  }

  const { error: updErr } = await supabase
    .from('orders')
    .update({ payment_proof_path: path })
    .in('id', orderIds)

  if (updErr) {
    console.error('[YFF] proof path save failed:', updErr.message)
    return bad('Saved screenshot but could not link it. Please contact support.', 500)
  }

  return NextResponse.json({ ok: true, path })
}
```

```
import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'

const supabaseUrl = process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!

---
'use client'

import { createContext, useCallback, useContext, useEffect, useRef, useState, ReactNode } from 'react'
import dynamic from 'next/dynamic'

export type Consumer = { id: string; name: string | null; phone: string }

type AuthState =
  | { status: 'loading'; consumer: null }
  | { status: 'authenticated'; consumer: Consumer }
  | { status: 'anonymous'; consumer: null }

type LoginPayload = { phone: string; password: string }
type RegisterPayload = { name: string; phone: string; password: string }
type AuthResult = { ok: true } | { ok: false; error: string }

type ConsumerAuthContextValue = {
  state: AuthState
  consumer: Consumer | null
  /**
   * Run `action` immediately when authenticated; otherwise open the auth modal
   * and run `action` after a successful login or registration.
   */
  requireAuth: (action: () => void) => void
  login: (payload: LoginPayload) => Promise<AuthResult>
  register: (payload: RegisterPayload) => Promise<AuthResult>
  logout: () => Promise<void>
  openAuth: () => void
  closeAuth: () => void
}

const STORAGE_KEY = 'REDACTED'
const AUTH_EVENT = 'yff:consumer-auth-change'

const ConsumerAuthContext = createContext<ConsumerAuthContextValue | null>(null)

// Modal is loaded only when first opened, keeping the initial consumer page light
const AuthModal = dynamic(() => import('@/components/consumer/AuthModal'), { ssr: false })

function readCachedConsumer(): Consumer | null {
  if (typeof window === 'undefined') return null
  try {
    const raw = localStorage.getItem(STORAGE_KEY)
    if (!raw) return null
    const parsed = JSON.parse(raw)
    if (parsed && typeof parsed === 'object' && parsed.id && parsed.phone) return parsed as Consumer
    return null
  } catch {
    return null
  }
}

function writeCachedConsumer(c: Consumer | null) {
  if (typeof window === 'undefined') return
  if (c) localStorage.setItem(STORAGE_KEY, JSON.stringify(c))
  else localStorage.removeItem(STORAGE_KEY)
  window.dispatchEvent(new Event(AUTH_EVENT))
}

export function ConsumerAuthProvider({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
  const [state, setState] = useState<AuthState>({ status: 'loading', consumer: null })
  const [modalOpen, setModalOpen] = useState(false)
  const pendingAction = useRef<(() => void) | null>(null)

  // Hydrate from cache instantly, then revalidate against the server cookie
  useEffect(() => {
    const cached = readCachedConsumer()
    if (cached) setState({ status: 'authenticated', consumer: cached })

    let cancelled = false
    fetch('/api/consumer/me', { credentials: 'same-origin' })
      .then((r) => r.json())
      .then((json) => {
        if (cancelled) return
        const c: Consumer | null = json?.consumer ?? null
        if (c) {
          writeCachedConsumer(c)
          setState({ status: 'authenticated', consumer: c })
        } else {
          writeCachedConsumer(null)
          setState({ status: 'anonymous', consumer: null })
        }
      })
      .catch(() => {
        if (cancelled) return
        if (!cached) setState({ status: 'anonymous', consumer: null })
      })

    const sync = () => {
      const next = readCachedConsumer()
      setState(next
        ? { status: 'authenticated', consumer: next }
        : { status: 'anonymous', consumer: null })
    }
    window.addEventListener(AUTH_EVENT, sync)
    window.addEventListener('storage', sync)
    return () => {
      cancelled = true
      window.removeEventListener(AUTH_EVENT, sync)
      window.removeEventListener('storage', sync)
    }
  }, [])

  const handleAuthSuccess = useCallback((consumer: Consumer) => {
    writeCachedConsumer(consumer)
    setState({ status: 'authenticated', consumer })
    setModalOpen(false)
    const action = pendingAction.current
    pendingAction.current = null
    if (action) {
      // Run on next tick so the modal can finish closing first
      setTimeout(action, 0)
    }
  }, [])

  const login = useCallback(async ({ phone, password }: LoginPayload): Promise<AuthResult> => {
    const r = await fetch('/api/consumer/login', {
      method: 'POST',
      headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
      credentials: 'same-origin',
      body: JSON.stringify({ phone, password }),
    })
    const json = await r.json().catch(() => ({}))
    if (!r.ok || !json?.ok || !json?.consumer) {
      return { ok: false, error: json?.error ?? 'Login failed.' }
    }
    handleAuthSuccess(json.consumer)
    return { ok: true }
  }, [handleAuthSuccess])

  const register = useCallback(async (payload: RegisterPayload): Promise<AuthResult> => {
    const r = await fetch('/api/consumer/register', {
      method: 'POST',
      headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
      credentials: 'same-origin',
      body: JSON.stringify(payload),
    })
    const json = await r.json().catch(() => ({}))
    if (!r.ok || !json?.ok || !json?.consumer) {
      return { ok: false, error: json?.error ?? 'Sign-up failed.' }
    }
    handleAuthSuccess(json.consumer)
    return { ok: true }
  }, [handleAuthSuccess])

  const logout = useCallback(async () => {
    await fetch('/api/consumer/logout', { method: 'POST', credentials: 'same-origin' }).catch(() => null)
    writeCachedConsumer(null)
    setState({ status: 'anonymous', consumer: null })
  }, [])

  const requireAuth = useCallback((action: () => void) => {
    if (state.status === 'authenticated') {
      action()
      return
    }
    pendingAction.current = action
    setModalOpen(true)
  }, [state.status])

  const openAuth = useCallback(() => setModalOpen(true), [])
  const closeAuth = useCallback(() => {
    pendingAction.current = null
    setModalOpen(false)
  }, [])

  return (
    <ConsumerAuthContext.Provider
      value={{
        state,
        consumer: state.consumer,
        requireAuth,
        login,
        register,
        logout,
        openAuth,
        closeAuth,
      }}
    >
      {children}
      {modalOpen && <AuthModal />}
    </ConsumerAuthContext.Provider>
  )
}

export function useConsumerAuth(): ConsumerAuthContextValue {
  const ctx = useContext(ConsumerAuthContext)
  if (!ctx) throw new Error('useConsumerAuth must be used inside <ConsumerAuthProvider>')
  return ctx
}
```

```
3	import { useEffect, useState, useCallback } from 'react'
4	import Link from 'next/link'
5	import { useLang } from '@/lib/LanguageContext'
6	import LanguageToggle from '@/components/LanguageToggle'
7	import { useConsumerAuth } from '@/lib/ConsumerAuthContext'
8	
9	type Order = {
10	  id: string
11	  produce_name: string | null
12	  quantity: number | null
13	  unit: string | null
14	  total_price: number | null
15	  pickup_location: string | null
16	  status: 'pending' | 'approved' | 'declined'
17	  payment_method: string | null
18	  payment_status: string | null
19	  decline_reason: string | null
20	  payment_proof_path: string | null
21	  created_at: string
22	  farmer_id: string
23	  farmer?: {
24	    name: string
25	    slug: string
26	    village: string
27	    phone: string | null
28	    upi_id: string | null
29	  } | null
30	}

32	export default function ConsumerOrdersPage() {
33	  const { tx } = useLang()
34	  const { state, openAuth } = useConsumerAuth()
35	  const [orders, setOrders] = useState<Order[]>([])
36	  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true)
37	  const [error, setError] = useState('')
38	  const [retryingId, setRetryingId] = useState<string | null>(null)
39	
40	  const refresh = useCallback(async () => {
41	    setLoading(true)
42	    setError('')
43	    const r = await fetch('/api/consumer/orders', { credentials: 'same-origin' }).catch(() => null)
44	    if (!r) { setError('Could not load orders. Check your connection.'); setLoading(false); return }
45	    const json = await r.json().catch(() => ({}))
46	    if (!r.ok) { setError(json?.error ?? 'Could not load orders.'); setLoading(false); return }
47	    setOrders((json.orders ?? []) as Order[])
48	    setLoading(false)
49	  }, [])
50	
51	  useEffect(() => {
52	    if (state.status === 'authenticated') {
53	      void refresh()
54	    } else if (state.status === 'anonymous') {
55	      setLoading(false)
56	    }
57	  }, [state.status, refresh])
58	
59	  const handleRetryPayment = async (order: Order) => {
60	    if (order.status === 'declined') return
61	    setRetryingId(order.id)
62	    const r = await fetch(`/api/orders/${order.id}/retry`, {
63	      method: 'POST',
64	      credentials: 'same-origin',
65	    }).catch(() => null)
66	    setRetryingId(null)
67	    if (!r) { setError('Network error.'); return }
68	    const json = await r.json().catch(() => ({}))
69	    if (!r.ok) { setError(json?.error ?? 'Could not retry payment.'); return }
70	
71	    setOrders((prev) => prev.map((o) =>
72	      o.id === order.id ? { ...o, payment_status: 'pending', payment_proof_path: null } : o,
73	    ))
74	    if (order.farmer?.upi_id && order.total_price) {
75	      const upiLink = `upi://pay?pa=${encodeURIComponent(order.farmer.upi_id)}&pn=${encodeURIComponent(order.farmer.name)}&am=${order.total_price}&cu=INR&tn=YourFamilyFarmer%20Order`
76	      window.location.href = upiLink
77	    }
78	  }
79	
80	  const statusColor = (s: string) =>
81	    s === 'approved'
82	      ? 'bg-green-100 text-green-800'
83	      : s === 'declined'
84	        ? 'bg-red-100 text-red-700'
85	        : 'bg-amber-100 text-amber-800'
86	
87	  const statusLabel = (s: string) =>
88	    s === 'approved' ? '✓ Confirmed' : s === 'declined' ? '✕ Declined' : '⏳ Pending'
89	
90	  const paymentBadge = (order: Order) => {
91	    if (!order.payment_method || order.payment_method === 'cod') return null
92	    if (order.payment_method === 'upi') {
93	      if (order.payment_status === 'completed') return { label: '✓ UPI Paid', cls: 'bg-green-100 text-green-800' }
94	      if (order.payment_status === 'pending_confirmation' || order.payment_status === 'payment_claimed')
95	        return { label: '⏳ Payment sent — awaiting farmer', cls: 'bg-blue-100 text-blue-800' }
96	      if (order.payment_status === 'failed') return { label: '✕ Payment not received', cls: 'bg-red-100 text-red-800' }
97	      return { label: '📲 UPI — Pay now', cls: 'bg-orange-100 text-orange-800' }
98	    }
99	    return null
100	  }
101	
102	  return (
103	    <main className="min-h-screen bg-gray-50 pb-16">
104	      {/* Header */}
105	      <div className="bg-green-900 px-4 pt-6 pb-10">
106	        <div className="flex items-center justify-between mb-4">
107	          <Link href="/consumer" className="text-green-300 text-sm flex items-center gap-1">
108	            ← Back / వెనక్కు
109	          </Link>
110	          <LanguageToggle />
111	        </div>
112	        <h1 className="text-white text-xl font-extrabold leading-tight">{tx.myOrders}</h1>
113	        <p className="text-green-400 text-sm mt-1">
114	          Your complete order history / మీ ఆర్డర్ల చరిత్ర
115	        </p>
116	      </div>
117	
118	      <div className="px-4 -mt-5 space-y-4 max-w-lg mx-auto">
119	        {state.status === 'loading' || loading ? (
120	          <div className="bg-white rounded-2xl border border-gray-100 p-6 text-center text-sm text-gray-500">
121	            Loading... / లోడ్ అవుతోంది
122	          </div>
123	        ) : state.status === 'anonymous' ? (
124	          <div className="bg-white rounded-2xl border border-gray-100 p-6 text-center space-y-4">
125	            <div className="text-5xl">🔒</div>
126	            <div>
127	              <p className="font-bold text-gray-900">Log in to see your orders</p>
128	              <p className="text-xs text-gray-500 mt-1">
129	                మీ ఆర్డర్‌లు చూడటానికి లాగిన్ అవ్వండి
130	              </p>
131	            </div>
132	            <button
133	              onClick={openAuth}
134	              className="w-full bg-green-700 text-white font-bold py-3.5 rounded-xl text-sm active:bg-green-800"
135	            >
136	              Log in / లాగిన్
137	            </button>
138	          </div>
139	        ) : error ? (
140	          <div className="bg-white rounded-2xl border border-red-100 p-6 space-y-3">
141	            <p className="text-sm text-red-600">{error}</p>
142	            <button
143	              onClick={() => void refresh()}
144	              className="text-sm font-bold text-green-700 underline"
145	            >
146	              Try again
147	            </button>
148	          </div>
149	        ) : orders.length === 0 ? (
150	          <div className="text-center py-14">
151	            <div className="text-5xl mb-3">📭</div>
152	            <p className="font-semibold text-gray-500 text-sm">{tx.noOrdersYet}</p>
153	            <Link href="/consumer" className="mt-4 inline-block text-green-700 text-sm underline font-semibold">
154	              Browse produce → / పంట చూడండి
155	            </Link>
156	          </div>
157	        ) : (
158	          <div className="space-y-3">
159	            <p className="text-xs font-semibold text-gray-500 uppercase tracking-wide">
160	              {orders.length} order{orders.length !== 1 ? 's' : ''} found
161	            </p>
162	            {orders.map((order) => {
163	              const badge = paymentBadge(order)
164	              const needsPayment = order.payment_method === 'upi'
165	                && order.payment_status === 'pending'
166	                && order.status !== 'declined'
167	                && order.farmer?.upi_id
168	              const canRetry = order.payment_method === 'upi'
169	                && order.payment_status === 'failed'
170	                && order.status !== 'declined'
171	                && !!order.farmer?.upi_id
172	                && !!order.total_price
173	              const upiLink = needsPayment
174	                ? `upi://pay?pa=${encodeURIComponent(order.farmer!.upi_id!)}&pn=${encodeURIComponent(order.farmer!.name)}&am=${order.total_price ?? 0}&cu=INR&tn=YourFamilyFarmer%20Order`
175	                : null
176	              return (
177	                <Link key={order.id} href={`/consumer/orders/${order.id}`} className="block">
178	                  <div className="bg-white rounded-2xl border border-gray-100 overflow-hidden active:bg-gray-50">
179	                    <div className="p-4 space-y-2">
180	                      <div className="flex items-start justify-between gap-2">
181	                        <div className="min-w-0">
182	                          <p className="font-extrabold text-gray-900 text-sm leading-tight">
183	                            {order.produce_name || '—'}
184	                          </p>
185	                          <p className="text-xs text-gray-500 mt-0.5">
186	                            {order.quantity} {order.unit || 'kg'}
187	                            {order.total_price ? ` · ₹${order.total_price}` : ''}
188	                          </p>
189	                        </div>
190	                        <div className="flex flex-col items-end gap-1 flex-shrink-0">
191	                          <span className={`text-[10px] font-bold px-2 py-0.5 rounded-full whitespace-nowrap ${statusColor(order.status)}`}>
192	                            {statusLabel(order.status)}
193	                          </span>
194	                          {badge && (
195	                            <span className={`text-[10px] font-bold px-2 py-0.5 rounded-full whitespace-nowrap ${badge.cls}`}>
196	                              {badge.label}
197	                            </span>
198	                          )}
199	                        </div>
200	                      </div>
201	
202	                      {order.farmer && (
203	                        <p className="flex items-center gap-1.5 text-xs text-green-700 font-semibold">
204	                          🧑‍🌾 {tx.orderedFrom} {order.farmer.name} · {order.farmer.village}
205	                        </p>
206	                      )}
207	
208	                      {order.pickup_location && (
209	                        <p className="text-xs text-gray-500">📍 {tx.pickedUpAt}: {order.pickup_location}</p>
210	                      )}
211	
212	                      <p className="text-[11px] text-gray-400">
213	                        {new Date(order.created_at).toLocaleDateString('en-IN', {
214	                          day: 'numeric',
215	                          month: 'short',
216	                          year: 'numeric',
217	                          hour: '2-digit',
218	                          minute: '2-digit',
219	                        })}
220	                      </p>
221	
222	                      {/* Decline reason — shown only when farmer declined */}
223	                      {order.status === 'declined' && order.decline_reason && (
224	                        <div className="bg-red-50 border border-red-200 rounded-xl px-3 py-2 mt-1">
225	                          <p className="text-[10px] font-bold text-red-700 uppercase tracking-wide">
226	                            Reason / కారణం
227	                          </p>
228	                          <p className="text-xs text-red-800 mt-0.5 leading-snug">{order.decline_reason}</p>
229	                        </div>
230	                      )}
231	
232	                      {/* Pay Now button for pending UPI orders */}
233	                      {needsPayment && upiLink && (

239	                          >

247

266	                          </button>
```

```
-- ================================================================
-- UPI Direct Payment Migration
-- Run in: Supabase Dashboard → SQL Editor → New Query → Run
-- ================================================================

-- 1. UPI ID for each farmer (used for direct buyer-to-farmer payment)
ALTER TABLE farmers ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS upi_id text;

-- 2. Screenshot proof URL on orders (optional buyer upload)
ALTER TABLE orders ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS payment_screenshot_url text;

-- 3. Index for faster payment status queries on orders
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_orders_payment_status ON orders(payment_status);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_orders_payment_method ON orders(payment_method);
-- ================================================================
-- YFF Feature Migration
-- Run in: Supabase Dashboard → SQL Editor → New Query → Run
-- ================================================================

-- 1. Harvest date on produce listings
ALTER TABLE produce_listings
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS harvest_date date;

-- 2. Cover photo for farmer profile banner
ALTER TABLE farmers
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS cover_photo_url text;

-- 3. Farmer avatar/profile photo
ALTER TABLE farmers
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS photo_url text;

-- 4. Pesticide test certificate (photo URL)
ALTER TABLE farmers
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS pesticide_cert_url text;

-- 5. Pickup schedule: {days: string[], time_from: string, time_to: string}
ALTER TABLE farmers
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS pickup_slots jsonb;

-- 6. OTP table for secure farmer login
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS farmer_otps (
  id          uuid        DEFAULT gen_random_uuid() PRIMARY KEY,
  phone       text        NOT NULL,
  otp         text        NOT NULL,
  expires_at  timestamptz NOT NULL,
  used        boolean     DEFAULT false,
  created_at  timestamptz DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_farmer_otps_phone   ON farmer_otps(phone);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_farmer_otps_expires ON farmer_otps(expires_at);

-- RLS: only service-role key (used in API routes) can access
ALTER TABLE farmer_otps ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;

-- 7. Payment method and status on orders (COD support)
ALTER TABLE orders ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS payment_method text DEFAULT 'cod';
ALTER TABLE orders ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS payment_status text DEFAULT 'pending';

-- 8. Farmer GPS location for distance-based discovery
ALTER TABLE farmers ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS lat numeric(10,7);
ALTER TABLE farmers ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS lng numeric(10,7);
ALTER TABLE farmers ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS location_name text;

-- 9. Password hash for farmer login
ALTER TABLE farmers ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS password_hash text;

-- 10. Farm gallery photos
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS media (
  id          uuid        DEFAULT gen_random_uuid() PRIMARY KEY,
  farmer_id   uuid        REFERENCES farmers(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  type        text        NOT NULL DEFAULT 'photo',
  url         text        NOT NULL,
  caption     text,
  sort_order  integer     DEFAULT 0,
  created_at  timestamptz DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_media_farmer_id ON media(farmer_id);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_media_type      ON media(type);

-- 11. Reviewer phone for duplicate-review prevention
ALTER TABLE reviews ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS reviewer_phone text;
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_reviews_farmer_phone ON reviews(farmer_id, reviewer_phone);

-- 12. WhatsApp click analytics (insert-only, no auth needed)
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS wa_clicks (
  id         uuid        DEFAULT gen_random_uuid() PRIMARY KEY,
  farmer_id  uuid        REFERENCES farmers(id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
  created_at timestamptz DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_wa_clicks_farmer ON wa_clicks(farmer_id);

-- Allow anonymous inserts for wa_clicks (consumer clicks)
ALTER TABLE wa_clicks ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
DO $$
BEGIN
  IF NOT EXISTS (
    SELECT 1 FROM pg_policies
    WHERE tablename = 'wa_clicks' AND policyname = 'anon insert wa_clicks'
  ) THEN
    EXECUTE 'CREATE POLICY "anon insert wa_clicks" ON wa_clicks FOR INSERT TO anon WITH CHECK (true)';
  END IF;
END $$;
-- ================================================================
-- Payment QR Code Migration
-- Run in: Supabase Dashboard → SQL Editor → New Query → Run
-- ================================================================

-- 1. UPI QR code image URL for each farmer
ALTER TABLE farmers ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS upi_qr_code_url text;

-- 2. Optional transaction reference / UTR number on orders
ALTER TABLE orders ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS utr_number text;
```
